# Lever registry (gpu02) -- every tested lever, its numbers and its status

> RISER MOVE 12.11. (~10:10 UTC, user hardware): gpu02 cards re-slotted -- 83:00 (4d61..) -> slot 6 (root c0), 03:00 (27d4..) -> slot 4 (root 40), c3:00 (dbba..) -> slot 2 on a riser (root 00); 86:00 (5943..) stays in its slot. NEW MAP (coordinator, read back after boot): dbba -> 03:00 (root 00, riser), 27d4 -> 48:00 (root 40), 5943 -> 83:00 (root 80), 4d61 -> c3:00 (root c0); all four 32 GT/s x16, 210 W, -50 mV, host tuning active (read-only check ~6 min after boot). Every bus address and the KFD order changed: '83:00' now names 5943.., not 4d61... Every Flash-Next figure from before the move (start-level tok/s, ms/step, prefill tok/s, TTFT; e.g. 160 c16 1031.7, 186 A arms, 173 x3arm) is NOT a cross-start reference afterwards -- only in-process A/B ratios from before stay valid as ratios. FN items pin cards by chip UUID (ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES), the one-card reads by GPU-dbba5c851cfef7b3 (the pre-move device 0).


Created 9.11.2026. Source of truth for the candidate stack: a lever that is not in this file is not decided. Update a row
whenever an item measures it (date, item, numbers with their reference). Status: IN STACK / CANDIDATE / PENDING (measurement
queued) / BUILDING / REJECTED (with the reason) / PARKED (could matter later) / AVOID (a default to override).
Exact = greedy output byte-identical to the reference line. cc = cc_sim u16 paired total time B/A [95 % CI];
"forced" = --gen-mode forced (acceptance-sensitive levers must be re-checked in natural mode, see TESTING.md 9.11.).

Cause: outputs are not batch-invariant (kernel forms follow the row count), so 9 of 16 users diverge on their FIRST turn and every later prompt is built from the run's own outputs; with --reflen-eos stop the lengths then differ by 3x. Needed for a valid MTP cc comparison: (1) the reference run records each request's generated text; (2) replay builds every run's history from the REFERENCE's outputs, so prompts are identical request for request; (3) open-loop arrivals at the reference's start times, so the concurrency profile is the same; (4) per-request paired rates (TTFT, decode tok/s, acceptance) on identical prompts, noise from an A/A replay pair. 126b held until then |

Runner rule (10.11., coordinator): offline analysis (stamp splits, gapcat, lag, threadcpu) runs after the prefill phases or in the background, so decode -> prefill stays back to back (112's pf16 ran on cards cooled to 30-33 C after ~4 min of analysis: 7782.4 tok/s vs ~7570 warm, +2.8 % -- 112's pf rows are COLD and not used here). The abblocks2 items (129-136) and 93o (132) already run the analysis after the arm.

Queue cc audit (10.11., coordinator): forced-mode cc pairs are invalid with MTP and natural / reflen pairs diverge (123), so the cc phases were removed from the not-started Flash-Next items -- 112 tp4pin (X112_CC_ARMS empty by default, ~1 h saved) and 110 dp2tuned (wrapper -> 110b with cc behind X110_CC=0, ~4-5 h saved); originals in gpu02 queue/held/. The DS items (57-p5 onsh, 57-p6b t3x-ab, 57-p7 ccpair-u8) run NST 0 (no drafting): their forced fixed-work pairs stay (valid, but ~+-6 % wide: 57-p1). 126b stays held until the replay method's A/A floor is in.

Clock vs co-running (10.11., from 127 / 95 / 130-134 / 132 data, no new run): the compute lane's in-server slowdown against one card alone is mostly CLOCK. 132's prefill windows ran 2254-2375 MHz (85-86 C, every card at its 210 W cap) against ~2786 MHz on cold cards (125) -- ~1.2x for compute-bound kernels. In-server / alone: hc_read 726 / 605 us = 1.20 (130, cold flush); hc_write 210 / 175.6 = 1.20; i8a8 ssm_inz 300.8 / 214.7 = 1.40, ssm_out 1.28, attn_output 1.44, shexp 1.18 (134 perfw8 is warm: MALL-resident operands, so part of the i8a8 residue is cold operands); attn_qg 301.4 / 156.4 = 1.93 is an outlier (not explained by clock or co-running). Co-running with the AR lane, from 95 (one lane, cold cards) vs 127 (two lanes, warm) per 2048 rows: hc_read +28 %, hc_write +13 %, i8a8 +5 %, MoE +1 % -- after the ~1.2x clock ratio between those runs' conditions this leaves ~0-6 % on the hc kernels and ~0 % elsewhere. Both AR-lane retunes lost ~3 % (A1), so the lane's throughput is exposed and its interference is small: priority / fewer-CU tricks for the AR can buy at most a few % of hc_read (~0.1-0.2 s at 128k). What can still buy the remaining +1.7..+2.9 % exactly: work per token on the compute lane at 210 W (U1 form 5: the up-mix stage, 227 of 546 us a 2048-row hc_read alone; h is read three times a PF2 call (126 MB), the MoE down's per-pick bf16 output round trip 210 MB a layer a piece), and fewer energy-hungry bytes, which raises the clock under the cap.

### Prefill and decode levers ranked from the per-op profiles (127 iq4r main mpB prefill, 120 EXL3 decode; 9.11.)

How the prefill step runs (qwen4exp step_pipelined): lane 0 = ALL compute of both 2048-row pieces, lane 1 = ALL
collectives (ti8 wire: production's --tp-wire wht6 at 128 KiB, tiered int8 at four ranks, 38 bytes / 32 values). So
the critical path is lane 0: 128k (119540 tok) lane 0 = 13879 ms busy + 672 ms idle of 15.04 s profiled (14.64 s
unprofiled); lane 1 = all_reduce 5958 ms + 8193 ms idle (waiting for lane 0's input: the join behind lane 0, not issue
delay, not peer waits -- those are inside the AR kernel). Of lane 0's idle, ~332 ms waits on the MoE all-reduce before
the last piece's hc_write, ~340 ms are per-op issue gaps of the profiled (live) issue (~4.4 us an op). 16k x4: lane 0
7057 ms busy + 554 idle; the first interval (never spanned) and the < 2048-row tail run single-lane, so their 96 ARs
each (444 ms over the four requests, 384 us a call) sit on the critical path. Bytes per rank, 2048-row piece.

The lab BOUNDED the idea: decode DELETED = -10.6 % of gate_up at M=2048 (-9.8 % at 4096, -3.5 % at 512; tp2, h_rot 1, del5) -- the most any reuse can buy; MTX 8 "saves 14 % of decodes at 2048, 31 % at 4096: <= -1.5 % / -3.0% before its own LDS and barrier costs -- NOT BUILT", MTX 8 NPW 1 built +26 % (217 VGPRs), NPW 2 spills; PAIR (decode once a tile an expert through LDS, 1bc6484) built, byte-identical, +22 % at 2048 / +31 % at 4096 (decode -> LDS -> barrier -> WMMA serialises a k-step). (a) at EP4: 6.55 MB of fp16 gate_up an expert, 839 MB a call for 128 experts -- written and read at 640 GB/s ~2.6 ms against the 0.53 ms call: fundamental (decoded weights are 4x the codes' bytes). VERDICT: (b) lost on implementation, but its ceiling is <= ~-5 % of gate_up at our ~24 rows an expert (<= ~-25 us a call, ~-0.06 s over 4 x 16k, < 1 %); (a) loses on bandwidth. Not a kernel limit: the kernel serves any M; its only shape refusal is ye rows or slot-table rows < M*top_k (2048 x 10), so the pair's per-piece gather (c02aa2c4 MoeFP8::pair_finish: ye = brows(w.edn, T*k), slots = brow_slice(w.inv, r0, rows, k)) passes an operand shorter than that -- an arch-side sizing bug, fix likely small, unverified (needs a repro). iq4r's P2x (3b88be82) shares pair_finish: same risk, never ran (190 stopped before its P2x copy). (no build): at K = 4 the adopted form (V1a packed pair hash, V1c, V1d; exl3r4d_common.hip.h decode_run) is 17 VALU a run of four weights -- one alignbit (s + 4) and two v_perm for the state pairs, two v_pk_mul_lo_u16 + two v_mad_u32_u16 per pair hash (x 2), four v_sad (the codebook's byte sums), two v_pk_fma -- ~4.25 a weight, all full rate; the byte sum needs the whole 32-bit hash (no packed 16-bit form gives its high half), and the pair hash already shares one multiply between two states. CORRECTION 12.11.: IMPLEMENTATION (large), not fundamental -- the decode kernels sum by __builtin_amdgcn_wmma_f32_16x16x16_f16 with split-K through LDS in wave order (exl3r4d.hip header, exl3r4d_common.hip.h:460); f16 x f16 products are exact in f32, so a host copy needs (a) a bit-model of gfx1201's WMMA sum of 16 products + c (our emulator's is explicitly NOT the hardware order, tests/r4d_emu/emu/hip/hip_runtime.h:153-156; exl3ref sums f32 ascending, ref.c:9-11 = another order), (b) exl3r4d's LDS split-K wave order, (c) the trellis decode (integer / f16, replicable). Without bit identity the answer depends on residency = on load -> a BI bug (user rule 11.11.). LINK SPLIT at c8 (stack5 S5T blocks b2T / b3T / b8T / b9T, nc8 regression, existing logs): per decode step 0.194-0.233 GB all ranks (~55 MB = ~6 units a rank), per newcomer step 28.4-38.8 GB; over a 45 s window decode steps carry 27-37 % of the link bytes (185-227 of 616-718 GB), newcomer steps 63-73 % (CPU useless there). DECODE-ONLY UPPER BOUND (estimate): ~55 MB a rank at ~12.7 GB/s = ~4.3 ms of a 36.3-36.6 ms decode step if wholly serial (in-kernel zero-copy reads; a layer's MoE waits for its slowest host unit) -> <= -12 % a decode step, c8 <= ~+10 % (decode time 35.3 -> 31.1 s of a 45 s window at share 0.785). Realistic far less (estimate): ~6 units a rank a step x ~2 ms CPU each (F2 est.) on 16 cores (EPYC 7302P) shared by 4 ranks, the result needed inside the same layer (~0.85 ms a layer) plus a host sync a layer -> 30-60 % of the bound, c8 ~+3..+6 %, 1u less (fewer misses). CHEAPEST CHECK: F2's measurement (per layer x rank missed units beside the per-op times) = the measured serial cost of one decode miss; only if that holds ~0.7 ms a miss, a one-card WMMA bit-model probe (crafted f16 fragments vs candidate sum orders) before any CPU kernel. (1) OUR SAMPLER IS ALREADY PER-POSITION COUNTER-BASED: p.seed + p.pos = n_out + dk (core/sample/sampler.cpp:823-826, philox rad_philox.h) -> draws equal with or without speculation; draft_sample 0 (greedy drafts verified against the seeded pick) is ALREADY draft-independent and batch-invariant at T > 0, only with lower acceptance. GC = a Gumbel-max pick (philox per (seed, pos, token)) so the drafter can share the noise; it changes the realised T > 0 samples vs today's sampler (same distribution) -> output change = the user's call (as SD was). (2) ACCEPTANCE (no draft / target logits logged): theory bound shared-noise >= (1 - TV) / (1 + TV) vs maximal coupling 1 - TV; SD sdtemp 11.11.: 1u essay T1 acc 0.671 (draft_sample 1) / 0.720 (85), 74.9 / 81.4 tok/s; at ~1.6-1.7 tok / step (stack4 / 5 T1 essay 1.56-1.58 tok / step) GC's bound acc >= 0.72 / 1.28 = 0.56 -> 1 + a: 1.72 -> >= 1.56 tok / step -> 1u essay T1 >= ~74 tok/s worst case, ~76-80 typical (estimate); i.e. GC buys T1 batch invariance at ~0..-9 % 1u essay T1 vs SD 85, and should beat draft_sample 0's greedy-draft acceptance (unmeasured at T1 on this stack). (3) BUILD ~4-6 agent hours: Gumbel-max sampler (philox per token over the filtered vocab), drafter walk using g on its candidates, verify rule, identity + acceptance item. USER DECISION 10.11. (via coordinator: "kyllä rakennetaan"): build the rank-independent slot-order MoE sum so that EP balancing (D3c, measured-cost placement) and hot-expert replication (F1) become byte-invariant to placement. The new sum is NON-EXACT ONCE vs today's line (rounding level: per-rank bf16 partials folded in rank order -> per-token slots summed in slot order), then PLACEMENT-INVARIANT. Includes a short quality check (KL on a staged corpus <= 15 min + the greedy set as information; no BetterBench, no cc). Default off (today's arithmetic); acceptance = byte-identical output across contiguous / measured-balanced / replicated placement (copy, deep, greedy c1) under the new sum.

## BI -- batch invariance (user 11.11.2026: answers changing under load are a bug; exactness includes batch invariance)
| item | scope | state |
|---|---|---|
| BI-x3 (EXL3 expert kernels, fn4's part) | above 16 tokens the routed-expert GEMMs switch from the decode band (exl3r4d.hip) to the prefill band (prefill.c), equal only to ~4e-3 rel L2; DS NST 0 under load (c4 + a 32k prefill) 6 / 20 greedy answers off the alone run | READING 177 QUEUED (gpu02 57-p4zyzzzzzz-b-fn-bicmp.sh; perfmoe 1206b8b --split-seed --x-bf16 --dump-h / --dump-y): per row, decode band M 1-16 vs prefill band M 8-2048, gate_up h and down y apart, Flash-Next K4 + DS K2 / K2.5. Code so far: both bands share prep_rotate (the input rotation) and the SwiGLU / scale / clamp expressions; suspects the K loop (split-K below 3 tokens, WMMA layout) and the epilogue Hadamard tiles. Then: the fix (prefill form bit-equal per row, or decode rows of mixed steps through the decode kernels -- costed in prefill tok/s and c4 / c16 decode), built behind a switch. Ownership proposed to the DS agent: all exl3r4d ops mine; router / top-k / scatter / gather / shared expert / attention / norms / wire theirs; Flash-Next hc_read / hc_write forms (M-band tiles, piece-row split-K, ti8 shards) offered 177b / 178 / 178b (11.11., one card, per row, bf16 x): PREFILL BAND M-INVARIANT (24 / 48 in 2048, 2048 in 2064 / 4096 / 4160, 512 in 2048: h and y 0 differ, Flash-Next K4 and DS K2 / K2.5); DECODE BAND M-invariant from M 3 (DS from M 1: its split-K fit check keeps split-K 1); gate_up's h THE SAME BITS in both bands; down's y DIFFERS across the band switch (Flash-Next K4 3 / 22 slots at M 8, 8 / 53 at M 16, max |d| 4; DS K2 3 / 17, 5 / 30, max 16; K2.5 0 / 17, 3 / 30, max 4) -- CAUSE (code): the decode down's epilogue takes EPIC (EXL3R4D_EPIC 1, adopted with "bits move inside the tolerance": the paired across-tile sum) while the prefill down (pf_epi_dn) is the plain EPI 2; and Flash-Next's decode gate_up at M 1 (h and y differ from M >= 2) takes split-K 2 (EXL3R4D_SK_SMALL_M 2; DS's units exceed the fit, so split-K 1).
